# Zero-configuration inventory

Snapshot: 2026-10-09, ticket TS-795. Each source location refers to the
repository's recorded baseline commit, before the policy PR. Repeated uses
are represented by the defining or first controlling location.

The operating contract is explained in
[How TerraScale runs with zero configuration](/fr/guides/zero-configuration).
Historical product switches remain for their owning removal threads. TS-796
owns orchestrator code removals; TS-773 owns TerraBase deploy-toggle removals.
TS-795 removes CI deployment and qualification opt-outs only.

**A source default is not a verified production value.** Unknown means no
live environment, process arguments or deployed configuration was read for
that entry. Required credentials are named, but their values are never
published. Names and baseline source locations are retained for completeness;
internal implementation descriptions and source defaults are withheld.
Private origins, addresses and personal paths are omitted.
The dashboard CI pause was verified as `1` on 2026-10-09; its workflow gate
is removed in TS-795.

This broad inventory also records tuning and discovery inputs, test-only
controls, vendor contracts, derived state and authorization or destructive
operation safeguards. The classification column distinguishes them from
product switches. A removal thread must preserve authentication, customer
data residency, billing consent and destructive-operation safeguards.
Vendor primitives are not permission to introduce TerraScale feature flags;
test controls belong only in test code and test binaries.
